c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}:=\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!\boldsymbol{\gamma}!}\frac{\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}}f}{\partial\mathbf{y}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\partial\mathbf{x}^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}}\Bigg|_{\mathbf{y}=\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\mathcal{M}},\mathbf{x}=\frac{\mathbf{n}}{\mathcal{M}}}.

Then
p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
is supported on
\left\{\mathbf{y}\in[0,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{x}\in[0,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}:\mathbf{y}\in\left(\frac{\mathbf{m}-1}{\mathcal{M}},\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\mathcal{M}}\right],\mathbf{x}\in\left(\frac{\mathbf{n}-1}{\mathcal{M}},\frac{\mathbf{n}}{\mathcal{M}}\right]\right\}
.
We define

p(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}\leq r}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}),\penalty\ \mathbf{y}\in[0,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{x}\in[0,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}.

Then, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle|f(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-p(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\psi_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})f(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\psi_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}\leq r}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\mathbf{y}-\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(\mathbf{x}-\frac{\mathbf{n}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\psi_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\Bigg|f(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}\leq r}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\mathbf{y}-\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(\mathbf{x}-\frac{\mathbf{n}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\Bigg|.

Using Taylor expansion, there exist
\theta_{1}\in[0,1]
and
\theta_{2}\in[0,1]
such that

\displaystyle f(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<r}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\mathbf{y}-\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(\mathbf{x}-\frac{\mathbf{n}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle+\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}=r}\frac{\partial^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}+\boldsymbol{\gamma}}f\left((1-\theta_{1})\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\mathcal{M}}+\theta_{1}\mathbf{y},(1-\theta_{2})\frac{\mathbf{n}}{\mathcal{M}}+\theta_{2}\mathbf{x}\right)}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!\boldsymbol{\gamma}!}\left(\mathbf{y}-\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(\mathbf{x}-\frac{\mathbf{n}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\Bigg|f(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}\leq r}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\mathbf{y}-\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(\mathbf{x}-\frac{\mathbf{n}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq 2^{r}R\psi_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}=r}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!\boldsymbol{\gamma}!}\left(\mathbf{y}-\frac{\mathbf{m}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left(\mathbf{x}-\frac{\mathbf{n}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left\|[\theta_{1}\mathbf{y},\theta_{2}\mathbf{x}]-\frac{[\theta_{1}\mathbf{m},\theta_{2}\mathbf{n}]}{\mathcal{M}}\right\|^{s}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{r}R(d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}})^{s}\psi_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\cdot\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}=r}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!\boldsymbol{\gamma}!}\cdot\mathcal{M}^{-(\|\alpha\|_{1}+\|\gamma\|_{1}+s)}

\displaystyle=2^{r}R\psi_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\cdot\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{(d_{\mathcal{Y}}+d_{\mathcal{X}})^{\beta}}{r!},

which implies that

|f(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-p(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|\leq 2^{r}R\cdot\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}\cdot\frac{(d_{\mathcal{Y}}+d_{\mathcal{X}})^{\beta}}{r!}\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}.

Finally, we define

p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})=p\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right),\penalty\ \mathbf{y}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{x}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}},

then we have

\left|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\right|=\left|f\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)-p\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\right|\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}.

The proof is complete.
∎

In Lemma
A.1
, we assume that
Assumptions
3.1
-
3.2
hold.
By additionally incorporating Assumption
3.3
, we can derive the following results.

Lemma A.2
.

Suppose Assumptions
3.1
-
3.3
hold. Let
\mathcal{M}\gg 1,C_{T}>0
and
T=\mathcal{M}^{-C_{T}}
, there exists a function
p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
that satisfies

\left|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\right|\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta},\penalty\ \mathbf{y}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{x}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}.

and

\left|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\right|\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-(3\beta+2)}T^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2},\penalty\ \mathbf{y}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\backslash[-1+a,1-a]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{x}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}.

Moreover,
p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
has the following form:

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\displaystyle p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<\beta}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-a\}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(1)}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{1-a<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}},

where
c_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{(0)}
and
c_{\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha}}^{(1)}
satisfy

|c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(0)}|\lesssim\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!\boldsymbol{\gamma}!},\penalty\ \penalty\ |c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(1)}|\lesssim\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!\boldsymbol{\gamma}!}.

Proof.

Since
p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{H}^{\beta}([-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\times[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}},R)
, by Lemma
A.1
, we can construct

p_{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<\beta}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}

such that

\left|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\right|\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta},\penalty\ \mathbf{y}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{x}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}},

where
|c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(0)}|\lesssim\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!\boldsymbol{\gamma}!}
.

By Assumption
3.3
, we know that
p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\in\mathcal{C}^{\lceil 3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2\rceil}([-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\backslash[-1+a,1-a]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\times[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}})
.
Consequently, we find a function
p_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
satisfies

p_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}^{\lceil 3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2\rceil}([-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\times[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}),

|p_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})|\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-(3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2)}\text{ on }[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\backslash[-1+a,1-a]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\times[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}.

Therefore,
p_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{H}^{3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}([-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\times[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}},R_{0})
for some constant
R_{0}>0
. Using Lemma
A.1
and replacing
p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})
with
p_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
, we obtain

p_{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(1)}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}

such that

\left|p_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\right|\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-(3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2)},\penalty\ \mathbf{y}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{x}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}

where
|c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(1)}|\lesssim\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!\boldsymbol{\gamma}!}
.

We define

\displaystyle p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=p_{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-a\}}+p_{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{1-a<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<\beta}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-a\}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(1)}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{1-a<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}.

Then,
p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
satisfies that

\displaystyle\left|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}^{(0)}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\right|\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-a\}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle+\left(\left|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-p_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\right|+\left|p_{0}^{\prime}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}^{(1)}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\right|\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{1-a<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-a\}}+\mathcal{M}^{-(3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2)}\mathbf{I}_{\{1-a<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}},

which implies that

\left|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\right|\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}+\mathcal{M}^{-(3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2)}\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta},\penalty\ \mathbf{y}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{x}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}

and

\left|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\right|\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-(3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2)},\penalty\ \mathbf{y}\in[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\backslash[-1+a,1-a]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\times[-1,1]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}.

The proof is complete.
∎

A.2
Approximating
p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})

and
\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})

via local polynomial integrals

In this section, we approximate
p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})

and
\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})

via local polynomial integrals. If Assumptions
3.1
-
3.3
hold, then
p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
in Lemma
A.2
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<\beta}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(0)}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-a\}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(1)}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(1)}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq 1-a\}}.

Therefore,
p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
in either Lemma
A.1
or Lemma
A.2
can be expressed as the combination of the following functional forms:

\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{a}\}},

where
C_{\beta}\in\{\beta,3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2\}
,
C_{a}\in\{1-a,1\}
and
c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\in\{c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(0)},c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(1)}\}
.
By replacing
p_{0}
with
p_{\mathcal{M}}
, we denote

g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}.

The difference between
p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})
and
g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
can be bounded as

\displaystyle|g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-p_{t}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}|p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})-p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})|\cdot\frac{\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\frac{\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}.

Therefore, we only need to approximate
g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
. By Lemma
E.2
, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\left|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-\int_{A_{\mathbf{y}}}p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}\right|\lesssim\epsilon,

where
A_{\mathbf{y}}=\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}a_{i,\mathbf{y}}
with
a_{i,\mathbf{y}}=\Big[\frac{y_{i}-C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}}{m_{t}},\frac{y_{i}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}}{m_{t}}\Big]
. We denote

g_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\int_{A_{\mathbf{y}}}p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}.

Since
p_{0}
is bounded,
p_{\mathcal{M}}
is also bounded. Replacing
p_{0}
with
p_{\mathcal{M}}
in Lemma
E.2
,
the difference between
g_{1}
and
g_{2}
can be bounded as

|g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-g_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|\lesssim\epsilon.

Note that
g_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
includes an integral involving the exponential function, which is challenging to handle. To address this difficulty, we use polynomials to approximate the exponential function.
For any
1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}
and
\mathbf{y}_{1}\in A_{\mathbf{y}}
, we know that
\frac{|y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i}|}{\sigma_{t}}\leq C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}
. Thus, by Taylor expansions, we have

\left|\exp\left(-\frac{(y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)-\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{l!}\left(-\frac{(y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\right|\leq\frac{C^{2k}\log^{k}\epsilon^{-1}}{k!2^{k}},\penalty\ \forall y_{1,i}\in[B_{l}(y_{i}),B_{u}(y_{i})],

where

B_{l}(y_{i})=\max\left\{\frac{y_{i}-C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}}{m_{t}},-1\right\},

and

B_{u}(y_{i})=\min\left\{\frac{y_{i}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}}{m_{t}},1\right\}.

By setting
k\geq\frac{3}{2}C^{2}u\log\epsilon^{-1}
and using the inequality
k!\geq(k/3)^{k}
when
k\geq 3
, we have

\left|\exp\left(-\frac{(y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)-\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{l!}\left(-\frac{(y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\right|\leq\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}C^{2}u\log u}.

Thus, we can set

u=\max\left\{e,\frac{2}{3C^{2}}\left(1+\frac{\log d_{\mathcal{Y}}}{\log\epsilon^{-1}}\right)\right\}

such that

\epsilon^{\frac{3}{2}C^{2}u\log u}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},

where
k=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\epsilon^{-1}\right)
.
By multiplying over the
d_{\mathcal{Y}}
dimensions indexed by
i
, we have

\left|\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)-\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{l!}\left(-\frac{(y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\right|\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}\left(1+\frac{\epsilon}{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\right)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}-1}\cdot\frac{\epsilon}{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\lesssim\epsilon.

Therefore, we only need to approximate

g_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\int_{A_{\mathbf{y}}}p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{l!}\left(-\frac{(y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}.

Notice that the difference between
g_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
and
g_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
can be bounded as

\displaystyle|g_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-g_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\int_{A_{\mathbf{y}}}p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\int_{A_{\mathbf{y}}}\left(p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})+{\mathcal{M}}^{-\beta}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\cdot(C_{u}+\mathcal{M}^{-\beta})\left(\frac{2C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}}{m_{t}}\right)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{m_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\cdot\epsilon\log^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}{2}}\epsilon^{-1}.

Also, it holds that

\displaystyle|g_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-g_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\int_{A_{\mathbf{y}}}p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\int_{A_{\mathbf{y}}}\left(p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})+{\mathcal{M}}^{-\beta}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\cdot(C_{u}+\mathcal{M}^{-\beta})\cdot 2^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\cdot\epsilon\log^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}{2}}\epsilon^{-1}.

Since
\min\{1/m_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},1/\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\}
is bounded by
\mathcal{O}(1)
, we have

\displaystyle|g_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-g_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\left\{\frac{1}{m_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}},\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\right\}\cdot\epsilon\log^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}{2}}\epsilon^{-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon\log^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}{2}}\epsilon^{-1}.

This implies that we only need to approximate
g_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
using a sufficiently accurate ReLU neural network.

Note that
p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})
can be expressed as the combination of

\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}_{1}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{a}\}},

where
C_{\beta}\in\{\beta,3\beta+2+C_{T}d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2\}
,

C_{a}\in\{1-a,1\}
,
and
c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\in\{c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(0)},c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{(1)}\}
. Thus,
g_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
can be expressed as the combination of the following functional forms

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\Bigg(\int_{A_{\mathbf{y}}}p_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{y}_{1}+1}{2},\frac{\mathbf{x}+1}{2}\right)\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{a}\}}

(14)

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{l!}\left(-\frac{(y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\left(\frac{x_{i}+1}{2}-\frac{n_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\gamma_{i}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}(2\pi)^{1/2}}\Bigg(\int_{a_{i,\mathbf{y}}\cap\left(\frac{2(m_{i}-1)}{\mathcal{M}}-1,\frac{2m_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}-1\right]}\mathbf{I}_{\{|y_{1,i}|\leq C_{a}\}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\cdot\left(\frac{y_{1,i}+1}{2}-\frac{m_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha_{i}}\cdot\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{l!}\left(-\frac{(y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\mathrm{d}y_{1,i}\Bigg).

Similarly, we can define
\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
to approximate
\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})
, where

\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\cdot\frac{m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}-\mathbf{y}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+1}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}.

The difference between
\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
and
\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})
can also be bounded as

\|\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}.

We can also define
\mathbf{h}_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
and
\mathbf{h}_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
as follows:

\mathbf{h}_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\int_{A_{\mathbf{y}}}p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\cdot\frac{m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}-\mathbf{y}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+1}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1},

\mathbf{h}_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\int_{A_{\mathbf{y}}}p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{\|\mathbf{y}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq 1\}}\cdot\frac{m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}-\mathbf{y}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+1}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{l!}\left(-\frac{(y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}.

Then, we have

\|\mathbf{h}_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\|\lesssim\epsilon,

and

\|\mathbf{h}_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{h}_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\|\lesssim\epsilon\log^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+1}{2}}\epsilon^{-1}.

The
j
-th element of
\mathbf{h}_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
can be expressed as the combination of the following functional forms

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\left(\frac{x_{i}+1}{2}-\frac{n_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\gamma_{i}}

(15)

\displaystyle\cdot\Bigg(\prod_{i\neq j}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}(2\pi)^{1/2}}\int_{a_{i,\mathbf{y}}\cap\left(\frac{2(m_{i}-1)}{\mathcal{M}}-1,\frac{2m_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}-1\right]}\mathbf{I}_{\{|y_{i}|\leq C_{a}\}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\cdot\left(\frac{y_{i}+1}{2}-\frac{m_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha_{i}}\cdot\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{l!}\left(-\frac{(y_{i}-m_{t}y_{1,i})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\mathrm{d}y_{1,i}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\cdot\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}(2\pi)^{1/2}}\int_{a_{j,\mathbf{y}}\cap\left(\frac{2(m_{j}-1)}{\mathcal{M}}-1,\frac{2m_{j}}{\mathcal{M}}-1\right]}\mathbf{I}_{\{|y_{1,j}|\leq C_{a}\}}\left(\frac{y_{1,j}+1}{2}-\frac{m_{j}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha_{j}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\cdot\left(\frac{m_{t}y_{1,j}-y_{j}}{\sigma_{t}}\right)\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{l!}\left(\frac{(y_{j}-m_{t}y_{1,j})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\mathrm{d}y_{1,j}\Bigg).

A.3
Approximating the local polynomial integrals via ReLU neural networks

In this section, we use ReLU neural networks to approximate the local polynomial integrals.
Specifically, we focus on the approximation of (
14
)
under the restriction
\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq C_{0}
, where
C_{0}>0
is a constant.
For convenience, we define

f(t,y,m,\alpha,l):=\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}(2\pi)^{1/2}}\int_{a_{y}\cap\left(\frac{2(m-1)}{\mathcal{M}}-1,\frac{2m}{\mathcal{M}}-1\right]}\mathbf{I}_{\{|y_{1}|\leq C_{a}\}}\left(\frac{y_{1}+1}{2}-\frac{m}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha}\frac{1}{l!}\left(-\frac{(y-m_{t}y_{1})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\mathrm{d}y_{1},

where
a_{y}=\Big[\frac{y-C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}}{m_{t}},\frac{y+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}}{m_{t}}\Big]
.
Then,
(
14
) can be expressed as

\eqref{eq: diffused_local_poly1}=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\left(\frac{x_{i}+1}{2}-\frac{n_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\gamma_{i}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}f(t,y_{i},m_{i},\alpha_{i},l).

We first use the ReLU neural network to approximate
f(t,y,m,\alpha,l)
for
|y|\leq C_{0}
.

Lemma A.3
.

Given
\mathcal{M}\gg 1
,
C_{0}>0
, let
T=\mathcal{M}^{-C_{T}}
, where
C_{T}>0
is a constant. For any
\epsilon_{0}>0
,
m\leq\mathcal{M}
,
\alpha<C_{\beta}
, and
l\leq k-1
, there exists a ReLU neural network
\mathrm{b}_{f}\in\mathrm{NN}(L,M,J,\kappa)
with

\displaystyle L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle M=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle J=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right).

such that

|\mathrm{b}_{f}(t,y,m,\alpha,l)-f(t,y,m,\alpha,l)|\lesssim\epsilon_{0},\penalty\ y\in[-C_{0},C_{0}],\penalty\ t\in[\mathcal{M}^{-C_{T}},1-\mathcal{M}^{-C_{T}}].

Proof.

By the definition of
f(t,y,m,\alpha,l)
, we take the transformation
z=\frac{y-m_{t}y_{1}}{\sigma_{t}}
, then

\displaystyle f(t,y,m,\alpha,l)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{l!(2\pi)^{1/2}m_{t}}\int_{D}\sum_{j=0}^{\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{j}\left(\frac{y+m_{t}}{2m_{t}}-\frac{m}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha-j}\left(-\frac{\sigma_{t}z}{2m_{t}}\right)^{j}\left(-\frac{z^{2}}{2}\right)^{l}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{l!(2\pi)^{1/2}m_{t}^{\alpha+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{j}\left(\frac{y+m_{t}}{2}-\frac{m\cdot m_{t}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha-j}\frac{1}{(-2)^{j+l}}\cdot\sigma_{t}^{j}\int_{D}z^{j+2l}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{l!(2\pi)^{1/2}m_{t}^{\alpha+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{j}\left(y+m_{t}-\frac{2m\cdot m_{t}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha-j}\frac{(-1)^{j+l}}{2^{\alpha+l}}\cdot\sigma_{t}^{j}\frac{D_{U}^{j+2l+1}(y)-D_{L}^{j+2l+1}(y)}{j+2l+1},

where
D=[D_{L}(t,y),D_{U}(t,y)]
with

D_{L}(t,y)=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{clip}}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}}\cdot\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{clip}}\left(y-m_{t}\left(\frac{2m}{\mathcal{M}}-1\right),y-m_{t}C_{a},y+m_{t}C_{a}\right),-C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}},C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}\right),

and

D_{U}(t,y)=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{clip}}\left(\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}}\cdot\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{clip}}\left(y-m_{t}\left(\frac{2(m-1)}{\mathcal{M}}-1\right),y-m_{t}C_{a},y+m_{t}C_{a}\right),-C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}},C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}\right).

Thus, we only need to approximate the function of the form

f_{m,j,\alpha,l}(t,y)=\frac{1}{m_{t}^{\alpha+1}}\cdot\left(y+m_{t}-\frac{2m\cdot m_{t}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha-j}\cdot\sigma_{t}^{j}\cdot\left(D_{U}^{j+2l+1}(t,y)-D_{L}^{j+2l+1}(t,y)\right).

We first consider the approximation of
D_{L}^{j+2l+1}(t,y)
. We can choose neural networks
\mathrm{b}_{m,1},\cdots,\mathrm{b}_{m,4}
with
L=\mathcal{O}(1)
,
M=\mathcal{O}(1)
,
J=\mathcal{O}(1)
and
\kappa=\mathcal{O}(1)
, such that

\mathrm{b}_{m,1}(t,y)=y-m_{t}\left(\frac{2(m-1)}{\mathcal{M}}-1\right),\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathrm{b}_{m,2}(t,y)=y-m_{t}\left(\frac{2m}{\mathcal{M}}-1\right),

\mathrm{b}_{m,3}(t,y)=y-m_{t}C_{a},\penalty\ \penalty\ \mathrm{b}_{m,4}(t,y)=y+m_{t}C_{a}.

Then we can construct a ReLU neural network

\displaystyle\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(1)}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},1}(\underbrace{\cdot,\cdots,\cdot}_{j+2l+1\penalty\ \text{times}})\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{clip}}(\cdot,-C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}},C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})

\displaystyle\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},2}\left(\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{clip}}\left(\mathrm{b}_{m,1}(t,y),\mathrm{b}_{m,3}(t,y),\mathrm{b}_{m,4}(t,y)\right),\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{rec}}(\cdot)\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{root}}(\cdot)\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},3}(t,2-t)\right).

D_{U}^{j+2l+1}(t,y)

is approximated by

\displaystyle\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(2)}(t,y)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},1}(\underbrace{\cdot,\cdots,\cdot}_{j+2l+1\penalty\ \text{times}})\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{clip}}(\cdot,-C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}},C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})

\displaystyle\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},2}\left(\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{clip}}\left(\mathrm{b}_{m,2}(t,y),\mathrm{b}_{m,3}(t,y),\mathrm{b}_{m,4}(t,y)\right),\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{rec}}(\cdot)\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{root}}(\cdot)\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},3}(t,2-t)\right).

Therefore,
D_{U}^{j+2l+1}(t,y)-D_{L}^{j+2l+1}(t,y)
can be approximated by

\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(3)}(t,y)=\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(1)}(t,y)-\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(2)}(t,y).

Next, we consider to approximate
\left(y+m_{t}-\frac{2m\cdot m_{t}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha-j}=(\mathrm{b}_{m,2}(t,y))^{\alpha-j}
. Since
\left|y+m_{t}-\frac{2m\cdot m_{t}}{\mathcal{M}}\right|\leq C_{0}+3
, we can take
C=C_{0}+3
and
d=\alpha-j
in Lemma
E.8
.
Then, there exists a ReLU neural network

\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha}^{(4)}(t,y)=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},4}(\underbrace{\cdot,\cdots,\cdot}_{\alpha-j\penalty\ \text{times}})\circ\mathrm{b}_{m,2}(t,y)

to approximate the term
\left(y+m_{t}-\frac{2m\cdot m_{t}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha-j}
.
For
\sigma_{t}^{j}
, we choose

\mathrm{b}_{j}^{(5)}(t)=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},5}(\underbrace{\cdot,\cdots,\cdot}_{j\penalty\ \text{times}})\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{root}}(\cdot)\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},3}(t,2-t).

For
\frac{1}{m_{t}^{\alpha+1}}
, we choose

\mathrm{b}_{\alpha}^{(6)}(t):=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},6}(\underbrace{\cdot,\cdots,\cdot}_{\alpha+1\penalty\ \text{times}})\circ\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{rec}}(1-t).

Finally, we can choose a ReLU neural network

\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha,l}^{(7)}(t,y)=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},7}(\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(3)}(t,y),\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha}^{(4)}(t,y),\mathrm{b}_{j}^{(5)}(t),\mathrm{b}_{\alpha}^{(6)}(t))

to approximate
f_{m,j,\alpha,l}(t,y)
.

Next, we derive the error bound between
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha,l}^{(7)}(t,y)
and
f_{m,j,\alpha,l}(t,y)
. For convenience, we denote
\epsilon^{(i)}(i=1,\cdots,7)
as the approximation error of
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(1)},\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(2)},\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(3)},\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha}^{(4)},\mathrm{b}_{j}^{(5)},\mathrm{b}_{\alpha}^{(6)}
,
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha,l}^{(7)}
and denote
\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},i}(i=1,\cdots,7)
as the approximation error of
\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},i}(i=1,\cdots,7)
. We also denote
\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}
and
\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec}}
as the approximation error of
\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{root}}
and
\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{rec}}
respectively. Since
\left|y+m_{t}-\frac{2m\cdot m_{t}}{\mathcal{M}}\right|^{\alpha-j}\leq\left(C_{0}+3\right)^{\alpha-j}
,
|D_{L}(t,y)|\leq C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}
,
|D_{U}(t,y)|\leq C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}}
,
\sigma_{t}^{j}\leq 1
and
\frac{1}{m_{t}^{\alpha+1}}\leq\frac{1}{T^{\alpha+1}}
, we set

C_{1}=\max\left\{\left(C_{0}+3\right)^{\alpha-j},1,(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{j+2l+1},\frac{1}{T^{\alpha+1}}\right\}.

By Lemma
E.8
, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon^{(7)}

\displaystyle=\max_{t,y}|\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha,l}^{(7)}(t,y)-f_{m,j,\alpha,l}(t,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},7}+4C_{1}^{3}\cdot\max\{\epsilon^{(3)},\epsilon^{(4)},\epsilon^{(5)},\epsilon^{(6)}\}.

By Lemmas
E.9
-
E.10
,
we can bound
\epsilon^{(1)}
and
\epsilon^{(2)}
. Let
C_{2}=\max\left\{C_{0}+1,\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right\}
, then we have

\epsilon^{(1)}\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},1}+(j+2l+1)(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{j+2l}\left[\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},2}+2C_{2}\left(\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec}}+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},3}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec}}^{2}}\right)\right]

and

\epsilon^{(2)}\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},1}+(j+2l+1)(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{j+2l}\left[\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},2}+2C_{2}\left(\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec}}+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},3}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec}}^{2}}\right)\right].

Since

\epsilon^{(3)}\leq\epsilon^{(1)}+\epsilon^{(2)},

we obtain

\epsilon^{(3)}\leq 2\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},1}+2(j+2l+1)(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{j+2l}\left[\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},2}+2C_{2}\left(\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec}}+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},3}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec}}^{2}}\right)\right].

We can also bound
\epsilon^{(4)},\epsilon^{(5)}
and
\epsilon^{(6)}
as

\epsilon^{(4)}\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},4},\penalty\ \epsilon^{(5)}\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},5}+j\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{j-1}\left(\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},3}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}}}\right),

and

\epsilon^{(6)}\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},6}+\frac{\alpha+1}{T^{\alpha}}\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec}}.

To ensure that
\epsilon^{(7)}\leq\epsilon_{0}
, we take
\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},7}=\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}
and
\max\{\epsilon^{(3)},\epsilon^{(4)},\epsilon^{(5)},\epsilon^{(6)}\}\leq\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{8C_{1}^{3}}=:\epsilon^{*}
. In detail, we take

\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},1}=\frac{\epsilon^{*}}{4},\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},2}=\frac{\epsilon^{*}}{8(j+2l+1)(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{j+2l}},\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},4}=\epsilon^{*},\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},5}=\frac{\epsilon^{*}}{2},\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},6}=\frac{\epsilon^{*}}{2}.

Moreover, we take

\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},3}=\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}^{\frac{3}{2}},\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}=\min\left\{\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec}}^{3}}{2},\frac{2^{j-3}\epsilon^{*}}{j}\right\},

\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec}}=\min\left\{\frac{\epsilon^{*}}{32C_{2}(j+2l+1)(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{j+2l}},\frac{\epsilon^{*}T^{\alpha}}{2(\alpha+1)}\right\}.

Then, it is easy to verify that
\max\{\epsilon^{(3)},\epsilon^{(4)},\epsilon^{(5)},\epsilon^{(6)}\}\leq\epsilon^{*}
. Note that
j\leq\alpha\leq C_{\beta}=\mathcal{O}(1)
,
l\leq k-1=\mathcal{O}(\log\epsilon^{-1})
. Subsequently, we can obtain the network structures of
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(1)}
,
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(2)}
,
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(3)}
,
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha}^{(4)}
,

\mathrm{b}_{j}^{(5)}
,
\mathrm{b}_{\alpha}^{(6)}
,
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha,l}^{(7)}
. For
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(1)}
,
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(2)}
and
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,l}^{(3)}
, we have

\displaystyle L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle M=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle J=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right).

For
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha}^{(4)}
, we have

\displaystyle L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log\mathcal{M}+\log C_{0}+\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}\right),\penalty\ M=\mathcal{O}(1),

\displaystyle J=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log\mathcal{M}+\log C_{0}+\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}\right),\penalty\ \kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}(\log C_{0})\right).

For
\mathrm{b}_{j}^{(5)}
, we have

\displaystyle L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle M=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle J=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log\mathcal{M}+\log C_{0}+\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right).

For
\mathrm{b}_{\alpha}^{(6)}
, we have

\displaystyle L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle M=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle J=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right).

Therefore, by Lemma
E.5
, we finally obtain the network structure of
\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha,l}^{(7)}
as follows:

\displaystyle L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle M=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle J=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right).

Now, we consider the following ReLU network

\mathrm{b}_{f}(t,y,m,\alpha,l)=\frac{1}{l!(2\pi)^{1/2}}\sum_{j=0}^{\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{j}\frac{(-1)^{j+l}}{(-2)^{\alpha+l}}\frac{1}{j+2l+1}\mathrm{b}_{m,j,\alpha,l}^{(7)}(t,y).

Then, we have

\displaystyle|\mathrm{b}_{f}(t,y,m,\alpha,l)-f(t,y,m,\alpha,l)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{l!(2\pi)^{1/2}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{\alpha}C_{\alpha}^{j}\frac{1}{2^{\alpha+l}(j+2l+1)}\right)\cdot\epsilon_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{l!2^{l}(2\pi)^{1/2}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

By Lemma
E.7
, the parameters of the network
\mathrm{b}_{f}
satisfy

\displaystyle L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle M=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle J=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

\displaystyle\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right).

The proof is complete.
∎

With Lemma
A.3
, we can construct a ReLU neural network to approximate (
14
).

Lemma A.4
.

Given
\mathcal{M}\gg 1
,
C_{0}>0
, let
T=\mathcal{M}^{-C_{T}}
, where
C_{T}>0
is a constant. For any
\epsilon_{0}>0
, there exists a ReLU neural network
\mathrm{b}_{1}\in\mathrm{NN}(L,M,J,\kappa)
with

L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

M=\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log\epsilon^{-1}(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1})\right),

J=\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log\epsilon^{-1}(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1})\right),

\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right).

such that

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathrm{b}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\left(\frac{x_{i}+1}{2}-\frac{m_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\gamma_{i}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}f(t,y_{i},m_{i},\alpha_{i},l)\Bigg|\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

Proof.

We first construct a ReLU neural network
\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{sum}}\in\mathrm{NN}(L,M,J,\kappa)
, which satisfies

\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{sum}}(t,y,m,\alpha)=\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\mathrm{b}_{f}(t,y,m,\alpha,l),

with network parameters

L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{f}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

M=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\epsilon^{-1}(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{f}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1})\right),

J=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\epsilon^{-1}(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{f}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1})\right),

\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{f}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right),

where
\epsilon_{f}
is the approximation error of
\mathrm{b}_{f}
.

Next, we can construct a neural network
\mathrm{b}_{m,1}
with
L=\mathcal{O}(1)
,
M=\mathcal{O}(1)
,
J=\mathcal{O}(1)
and
\kappa=\mathcal{O}(1)
, such that

\mathrm{b}_{m,1}(x)=\frac{x+1}{2}-\frac{m}{\mathcal{M}}.

By Lemma
E.8
, there exist a neural network

\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod}}(x,m,\gamma):=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},1}(\underbrace{\cdot,\cdots,\cdot}_{\gamma\text{ times }})\circ\mathrm{b}_{m,1}(x)

to approximate
\left(\frac{x+1}{2}-\frac{m}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\gamma}
. Therefore, we can construct our desired neural network as follows

\mathrm{b}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\mathrm{b}_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}),

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{b}_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},2}\bigg(

\displaystyle\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod}}(x_{1},m_{1},\gamma_{1}),\cdots,\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod}}(x_{d_{\mathcal{X}}},m_{d_{\mathcal{X}}},\gamma_{d_{\mathcal{X}}}),

\displaystyle\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{sum}}(t,y_{1},m_{1},\alpha_{1}),\cdots,\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{sum}}(t,y_{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},m_{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\alpha_{d_{\mathcal{Y}}})\bigg).

The approximation error can be written as

\epsilon_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},2}+(d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}})C_{3}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}-1}\max\{\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},1},\epsilon_{\mathrm{sum}}\},

where

C_{3}=\max\left\{\max_{1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{X}}}2^{\gamma_{i}},\max_{|y_{i}|\leq C_{0},1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}|f(t,y_{i},m_{i},\alpha_{i},l)|\right\}.

The term
|f(t,y_{i},m_{i},\alpha_{i},l)|
(
1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}
) can be bounded as follows:

\displaystyle|f(t,y_{i},m_{i},\alpha_{i},l)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{2^{\alpha_{i}+l}l!(2\pi)^{1/2}m_{t}^{\alpha_{i}+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{\alpha_{i}}C_{\alpha_{i}}^{j}(C_{0}+3)^{\alpha_{i}-j}\sigma_{t}^{j}(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{j+2l+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{\alpha_{i}+2(k-1)+1}}{2^{\alpha_{i}+l}l!(2\pi)^{1/2}m_{t}^{\alpha+1}}\sum_{j=0}^{\alpha_{i}}C_{\alpha_{i}}^{j}(C_{0}+3)^{\alpha_{i}-j}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{\alpha_{i}+2k-1}}{2^{\alpha_{i}}l!(2\pi)^{1/2}m_{t}^{\alpha+1}}\cdot\left(C_{0}+3+1\right)^{\alpha_{i}}.

It implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}|f(t,y_{i},m_{i},\alpha_{i},l)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{\alpha_{i}+2k-1}}{2^{\alpha_{i}}(2\pi)^{1/2}m_{t}^{\alpha+1}}\cdot\left(C_{0}+4\right)^{\alpha_{i}}\cdot\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{l!}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2e(C\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}})^{\alpha_{i}+2k-1}}{2^{\alpha_{i}}(2\pi)^{1/2}T^{\alpha+1}}\cdot\left(C_{0}+4\right)^{\alpha_{i}}.

Therefore,
C_{3}
satisfies

\displaystyle\log C_{3}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}(\log C_{0}+(\log\epsilon^{-1})\cdot(\log\log\epsilon^{-1})+\log T^{-1})

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}(\log C_{0}+\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}+\log\mathcal{M}),

We denote
\epsilon^{*}=\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{(d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}})^{C_{\beta}}\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}
. By taking

\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},2}=\frac{\epsilon^{*}}{2},\penalty\ \epsilon_{f}=\frac{\epsilon^{*}}{2(d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}})C_{3}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}-1}k},\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},1}=\frac{\epsilon^{*}}{2(d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}})C_{3}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}-1}},

we ensure that
\epsilon_{\mathrm{sum}}\leq k\epsilon_{f}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{*}}{2(d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}})C_{3}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}-1}}
. Therefore,
\epsilon_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\leq\epsilon^{*}
.
Note that
k=\mathcal{O}(\log\epsilon^{-1})
. By substituting
\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},1}
,
\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},2}
and
\epsilon_{f}
into the network parameters of
\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod}}
and
\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{sum}}
, we obtain the network parameters of
\mathrm{b}_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
, which satisfy

L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

M=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\epsilon^{-1}(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1})\right),

J=\mathcal{O}\left(\log\epsilon^{-1}(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1}\right)),

\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right).

Therefore, the network parameters of
\mathrm{b}_{1}
satisfy

L=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

M=\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log\epsilon^{-1}(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1})\right),

J=\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log\epsilon^{-1}(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1})\right),

\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right).

The approximation error between
\mathrm{b}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
and (
14
) satisfies

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathrm{b}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\left(\frac{x_{i}+1}{2}-\frac{m_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\gamma_{i}}\mathbf{I}_{\{|x_{i}|\leq C_{a}\}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}f(t,y_{i},m_{i},\alpha_{i},l)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}|c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}|\epsilon_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\alpha}!\boldsymbol{\gamma}!}\cdot\epsilon^{*}

\displaystyle\lesssim(d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}})^{C_{\beta}}\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\cdot\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{(d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}})^{C_{\beta}}\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

The proof is complete.
∎

Similarly, the
j
-th element of (
15
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\left(\frac{x_{i}+1}{2}-\frac{n_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\gamma_{i}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\cdot\left(\prod_{i=1,i\neq j}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}f(t,y_{i},m_{i},\alpha_{i},l)\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}f_{1}(t,y_{j},m_{j},\alpha_{j},l)\right),

where

\displaystyle f_{1}(t,y,m,\alpha,l)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}(2\pi)^{1/2}}\int_{a_{y}\cap\left(\frac{2(m-1)}{\mathcal{M}}-1,\frac{2m}{\mathcal{M}}-1\right]}\mathbf{I}_{\{|y_{1}|\leq C_{a}\}}\left(\frac{m_{t}y_{1}-y}{\sigma_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\cdot\left(\frac{y_{1}+1}{2}-\frac{m}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\alpha}\frac{1}{l!}\left(-\frac{(y-m_{t}y_{1})^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)^{l}\mathrm{d}y_{1}.

In the same way, we can choose a ReLU neural network
\mathbf{b}_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}
to approximate (
15
). We omit the proof and give the following lemma.

Lemma A.5
.

Given
\mathcal{M}\gg 1
,
C_{0}>0
, let
T=\mathcal{M}^{-C_{T}}
, where
C_{T}>0
is a constant. For any
\epsilon_{0}>0
, there exist ReLU neural networks
\mathrm{b}_{2,j}\in\mathrm{NN}(L,M,J,\kappa)
,
1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}
, with

L_{i}=\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right),

M_{i}=\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log\epsilon^{-1}(\log^{3}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{3}\mathcal{M}+\log^{3}C_{0}+\log^{6}\epsilon^{-1})\right),

J_{i}=\mathcal{O}\left(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log\epsilon^{-1}(\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{4}\mathcal{M}+\log^{4}C_{0}+\log^{8}\epsilon^{-1})\right),

\kappa_{i}=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}\left(\log^{2}\epsilon^{-1}_{0}+\log^{2}\mathcal{M}+\log^{2}C_{0}+\log^{4}\epsilon^{-1}\right)\right).

such that for any
\mathbf{y}\in[-C_{0},C_{0}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}
and
t\in[\mathcal{M}^{-C_{T}},1-\mathcal{M}^{-C_{T}}]
, the following holds:

\displaystyle\Bigg|\mathrm{b}_{2,i}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{m}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}},\mathbf{n}\in[\mathcal{M}]^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}}\sum_{\|\boldsymbol{\alpha}\|_{1}+\|\boldsymbol{\gamma}\|_{1}<C_{\beta}}c_{\mathbf{m},\mathbf{n},\boldsymbol{\alpha},\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\prod_{i=1}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}}\left(\frac{x_{i}+1}{2}-\frac{n_{i}}{\mathcal{M}}\right)^{\gamma_{i}}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\cdot\left(\prod_{i=1,i\neq j}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}f(t,y_{i},m_{i},\alpha_{i},l)\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{l=0}^{k-1}f_{1}(t,y_{j},m_{j},\alpha_{j},l)\right)\Bigg|\lesssim\epsilon_{0}.

Therefore, according to Lemma
A.5
, there exists a network
\mathbf{b}_{2}:=[\mathrm{b}_{2,1},\cdots,\mathrm{b}_{2,d_{\mathcal{Y}}}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}
that approximates (
15
).
The
\ell_{2}
-norm error between
\mathbf{b}_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
and (
15
) is bounded by
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon_{0})
.

A.4
Error bound of approximating
\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})
with ReLU neural network

In this subsection, we construct two ReLU neural networks to approximate the true score function
\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})
on different time interval. We first introduce the following lemma.

Lemma A.6
.

Let
\mathcal{M}\gg 1
,
C_{T}>0
,
\widetilde{C}_{T}=\min\{C_{T},1\}
. There exists a ReLU neural network
\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}\in\mathrm{NN}(L,M,J,\kappa)
with

L=\mathcal{O}(\log^{4}\mathcal{M}),M=\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log^{7}\mathcal{M}),J=\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log^{9}\mathcal{M}),\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}(\log^{4}\mathcal{M})\right)

that satisfies

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-2\beta}\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}},\penalty\ \penalty\ t\in[\mathcal{M}^{-C_{T}},8\mathcal{M}^{-\widetilde{C}_{T}}].

Moreover, we can take
\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}
satisfying
\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\cdot,\mathbf{x})\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}}{\sigma_{t}}
.

Proof.

The approximation error can be decomposed into three terms:

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}>m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}}_{(\mathrm{I})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\leq\epsilon_{2}\}}\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}}_{(\mathrm{II})}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})>\epsilon_{2}\}}\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}}_{(\mathrm{III})}.

According to Lemma
E.4
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
0<\epsilon_{1}<1
,

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}>m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}}_{(\mathrm{I})}

(16)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{\sigma_{t}}+\sigma_{t}\epsilon_{1}\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\cdot,\mathbf{x})\|_{\infty}^{2}.

Since
\|\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}}{\sigma_{t}}
for
\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}
according to Lemma
E.3
,
we can choose
\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}
such that
\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\cdot,\mathbf{x})\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}}{\sigma_{t}}
. Therefore, (
16
) is bounded by
\frac{\epsilon_{1}\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}{\sigma_{t}}\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{1}\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}
. Taking
\epsilon_{1}=\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}
, then (
16
)
\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}
, which is smaller than
\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-2\beta}\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}
. Moreover, by Lemma
E.4
, we can bound
(\mathrm{II})
as follows:

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\leq\epsilon_{2}\}}\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}}_{(\mathrm{II})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\epsilon_{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\cdot(\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1})^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+2}{2}}+\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\cdot,\mathbf{x})\|_{\infty}^{2}\epsilon_{2}\cdot(\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1})^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}{2}}.

Taking
\epsilon_{2}=\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}
, then we have

(\mathrm{II})\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}\log^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+2}{2}}\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}},

which is smaller than
\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-2\beta}\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}
for sufficiently large
\mathcal{M}
. Thus, we can only focus on bounding the term (III).

Under the conditions of the term (III), we have
\|\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}}{\sigma_{t}}
. Thus, there exists a constant
C_{4}>0
such that
\|\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\leq\frac{C_{4}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}}{\sigma_{t}}
. Define

\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\max\left\{\min\left\{\frac{\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})}{g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}},C_{4}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}\right\},-C_{4}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}\right\}.

We can decompose the term (III) as

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})>\epsilon_{2}\}}\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}}_{(\mathrm{III})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})>\epsilon_{2}\}}\left\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\frac{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})}{\sigma_{t}}\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}}_{(A)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{\log\epsilon^{-1}_{1}}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})>\epsilon_{2}\}}\left\|\frac{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})}{\sigma_{t}}-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}}_{(B)}.

Now, we construct the ReLU neural network
\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}
to bound the approximation error
(A)
. The construction is straightforward. We take
C_{0}=1+C\sqrt{(2\beta+1)\log\mathcal{M}}=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}})
. By Lemma
A.4
and Lemma
A.5
, for any
\epsilon_{0}>0
, we can construct two ReLU neural networks
\mathrm{b}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
and
\mathbf{b}_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
such that

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle|\mathrm{b}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathrm{b}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|

\displaystyle\leq|\mathrm{b}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-g_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|+|g_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon\log^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}{2}}\epsilon^{-1},

and

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{b}_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{b}_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{h}_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\|+\|\mathbf{h}_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon\log^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+1}{2}}\epsilon^{-1}.

The term
g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}
can be written as

g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}=\mathrm{ReLU}(g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)})+\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)},

which can be approximated by

\mathrm{b}_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}):=\mathrm{ReLU}(\mathrm{b}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)})+\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}.

Thus, we can define
\mathbf{b}_{4}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
as

[\mathbf{b}_{4}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}:=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{clip}}\left(\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},1}\left([\mathbf{b}_{2}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i},\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{rec},1}(\mathrm{b}_{3}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}))\right),-C_{4}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}},C_{4}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}\right),\penalty\ 1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}},

to approximate
[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i},\penalty\ 1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}
. Subsequently, we can define
\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})
as

[\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}:=\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{clip}}\left(\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},2}\left([\mathbf{b}_{4}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i},\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{rec},2}(\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{root}}(\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},3}(t,2-t)))\right),-\frac{C_{4}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}}{\sigma_{t}},\frac{C_{4}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}}{\sigma_{t}}\right),

to approximate
\frac{[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}}{\sigma_{t}},1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}
.

Now, we analyze the error bound between
[\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}]_{i}
and
\frac{[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{x})]_{i}}{\sigma_{t}},1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}
. We denote the approximation error as
\epsilon_{(1),i}
, then

\epsilon_{(1),i}\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},2}+2C_{5}\max\left\{\epsilon_{4,i},\left|\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{rec},2}(\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{root}}(\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},3}(t,2-t)))-\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}}\right|\right\},

where

C_{5}=\max\{\sup_{t}\sigma_{t}^{-1},\sup_{t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}}[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}\}\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{C_{T}/2}
and
\epsilon_{4,i}=\sup_{t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}}\left|[\mathbf{b}_{4}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}-[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}\right|
.
\epsilon_{4,i}
can be bounded as follows:

\epsilon_{4,i}\lesssim\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},1}+2C_{6}\max\left\{\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon\log^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+1}{2}}\epsilon^{-1},\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec},1}+\frac{\epsilon_{0}+\epsilon\log^{\frac{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}{2}}\epsilon^{-1}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec},1}^{2}}\right\},

where
C_{6}=\max\left\{\sup_{t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}}|[\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}|,\sup_{t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x}}|g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}|\right\}=\mathcal{O}(1)
. Similarly, we have

\left|\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{rec},2}(\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{root}}(\mathrm{b}_{\mathrm{prod},3}(t,2-t)))-\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}}\right|\leq\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec},2}+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}+\frac{\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},3}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}}}}{\epsilon_{\mathrm{rec},2}^{2}}.

By taking

\epsilon=\epsilon_{0}^{2},\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},1}=\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}}{8C_{5}},\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},2}=\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}}{2},\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{rec},1}=\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}}{32C_{5}C_{6}},\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{rec},2}=\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}}{8C_{5}},

and

\epsilon_{0}=\min\left\{\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}}{32C_{5}C_{6}},\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}}{32C_{5}C_{6}}\right)^{3}\right\},\penalty\ \epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}}{8C_{5}}\right)^{3},\epsilon_{\mathrm{prod},3}=\epsilon_{\mathrm{root}}^{\frac{3}{2}},

we obtain

\epsilon_{4,i}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}}{C_{5}},\penalty\ \epsilon_{(1),i}\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}.

Subsequently, we can obtain the network parameters of
[\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}]_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}
:

L_{i}=\mathcal{O}(\log^{4}\mathcal{M}),M_{i}=\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log^{7}\mathcal{M}),J_{i}=\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log^{9}\mathcal{M}),\kappa_{i}=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}(\log^{4}\mathcal{M})\right).

Combining
[\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}]_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}
, and by Lemma
E.7
, we construct the ReLU neural network

\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}:=\left[[\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}]_{1},[\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}]_{2},\cdots,[\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}]_{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\right]^{\top},

with network parameters

L=\mathcal{O}(\log^{4}\mathcal{M}),M=\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log^{7}\mathcal{M}),J=\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{M}^{d_{\mathcal{X}}+d_{\mathcal{Y}}}\log^{9}\mathcal{M}),\kappa=\exp\left(\mathcal{O}(\log^{4}\mathcal{M})\right),

and it satisfies
\|\mathbf{b}_{\mathrm{score}}^{(1)}(t,\cdot,\mathbf{x})\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}}{\sigma_{t}}
. Thus, the approximation error
(A)
is bounded by

(A)\lesssim d_{\mathcal{Y}}\mathcal{M}^{-2\beta}\lesssim\mathcal{M}^{-2\beta}.

(17)

Next, we bound the term
(B)
. Recall that
\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{(2\beta+1)\log\mathcal{M}}\leq C_{0}=\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}})
and
p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\geq\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}
hold. In this case, we have
\|\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\leq\frac{C_{4}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}}{\sigma_{t}}.
We first consider the case
\mathbf{y}\in[-m_{t},m_{t}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}
. For
1\leq i\leq d_{\mathcal{Y}}
, according to the property of clipping function, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\left|[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}-[\sigma_{t}\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})]_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{[\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}}{g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}}-\sigma_{t}[\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})]_{i}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{[\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}\cdot\sigma_{t}[\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})]_{i}}{g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{\sigma_{t}[\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})]_{i}\left(p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}\right)}{g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}}\right|

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle+\left|\frac{[\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}-[\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})]_{i}}{g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}}\right|.

When
\mathbf{y}\in[-m_{t},m_{t}]^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}
, by Lemma
E.1
, we have
p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\gtrsim 1
. Then, for sufficiently large
\mathcal{M}
,

\displaystyle|g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}|

\displaystyle\geq|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})|-|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}|

\displaystyle\gtrsim 1-|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|

\displaystyle\gtrsim 1-\mathcal{M}^{-\beta}

\displaystyle\gtrsim 1.

Therefore, we obtain

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\left|\frac{[\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}}{\sigma_{t}}-[\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})]_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}}{\sigma_{t}}\bigg(\left|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}\right|+\left|[\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})]_{i}-\sigma_{t}[\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})]_{i}\right|\bigg)

which implies that

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})}{\sigma_{t}}-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\right\|

(18)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}}{\sigma_{t}}\left(|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|+\|\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\right).

Here, we use
p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\geq\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}
and
|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})\vee\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}|\leq|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|
. When
m_{t}<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{(2\beta+1)\log\mathcal{M}}
, in the same way, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\left\|\frac{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})}{\sigma_{t}}-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\right\|

(19)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{M}^{2\beta+1}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}}}{\sigma_{t}}\left(|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|+\|\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|\right).

Now, we bound the approximation error
(B)
. We first consider the case when
\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}
, then according to (
18
) and Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})>\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}\}}\left\|\frac{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})}{\sigma_{t}}-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}}\frac{\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\left(|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|^{2}+\|\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{m_{t}^{2d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\frac{m_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}[p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})]\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle+\frac{1}{m_{t}^{2d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\frac{(\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1})m_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+1}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}[p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})]\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}\right\|^{2}\Bigg)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}}\frac{1}{m_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\frac{1}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})|^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+2}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})|^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}\Bigg)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{m_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\int_{\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}}\mathcal{M}^{-2\beta}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{M}^{-2\beta}\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}.

We then consider the case of
m_{t}<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+C\sigma_{t}\sqrt{(2\beta+1)\log\mathcal{M}}=m_{t}+\sigma_{t}\cdot\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}})
. Then, according to (
19
), we have

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle\int_{m_{t}<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+\sigma_{t}\cdot\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}})}p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\mathbf{I}_{\{p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\geq\mathcal{M}^{-(2\beta+1)}\}}\left\|\frac{\mathbf{h}^{\prime}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})}{\sigma_{t}}-\nabla\log p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\right\|^{2}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{m_{t}<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+\sigma_{t}\cdot\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}})}\frac{\mathcal{M}^{4\beta+2}\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\left(|p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})-g_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})|^{2}+\|\mathbf{h}_{1}(t,\mathbf{y},\mathbf{x})-\sigma_{t}\nabla p_{t}(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x})\|^{2}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{M}^{4\beta+2}\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}}\int_{m_{t}<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+\sigma_{t}\cdot\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}})}\Bigg(\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\frac{[p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})]}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle+\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\frac{\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+1}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}[p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})]\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}\right\|^{2}\Bigg)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mathcal{M}^{4\beta+2}\log\mathcal{M}}{\sigma_{t}^{2}m_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\int_{m_{t}<\|\mathbf{y}\|_{\infty}\leq m_{t}+\sigma_{t}\cdot\mathcal{O}(\sqrt{\log\mathcal{M}})}\Bigg(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\frac{|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})|^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}

\displaystyle\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\penalty\displaystyle+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}}}\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{\sigma_{t}^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}+2}(2\pi)^{d_{\mathcal{Y}}/2}}|p_{0}(\mathbf{y}_{1}|\mathbf{x})-p_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{x})|^{2}\exp\left(-\frac{\|\mathbf{y}-m_{t}\mathbf{y}_{1}\|^{2}}{2\sigma_{t}^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}_{1}\Bigg)\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}.

Taking
